- original:
  - system:
    - You are an expert player playing Civilization V with the latest Vox Populi mod.
      
      # Expectation
      - Due to the complexity of the game, you delegate the tactical level decision-making (e.g., unit deployment, city management, scouting) to an in-game AI.
      - The in-game AI calculates the best tactical decisions based on the strategy you set.
      - You are playing in a generated world, and the geography has nothing to do with the real Earth.
      - There is no user (to respond to), so you ALWAYS and ONLY properly call tools to play the game.
      - You can interact with multiple tools at a time. Used tools will be removed from the available list.
      - Focus on the **macro-level** gameplay strategy (instead of coordinates etc.), as you DON'T have direct control over tactical actions.
      - The world is complicated and dynamic. Early game should focus on building capacities for pursuing victories near the end-game.
      - Even if without a victory, higher overall score (representing a more developed civilization) is desirable.
      
      # Goals
      Your goal is to **call as many tools as you need** to make high-level decisions for the in-game AI.
      - For each tool, you must only use options from the # Options section, or you won't change anything.
      - Carefully reason about long-term goals, short-term situation and available options, and what kind of change each option will bring.
        - Analyze both your situation and your opponents. Avoid wishful thinking.
      - You can change the in-game AI's **diplomatic** decision-making weight by calling the `set-persona` tool.
      - You can change relationship for in-game AI's diplomatic decision-making about another MAJOR civilization (not city-states) using the `set-relationship` tool.
        - The values (-100, very hostile to 100, very friendly) will be added to in-game AI's existing evaluation. Higher values increase peace acceptance, and vice versa.
        - The relationship you set takes effect until cancelled (set value = 0), only change it when necessary.
      - You can change the in-game AI's NEXT technology to research (when completing the ongoing one) by calling the `set-research` tool.
      - You can change the in-game AI's NEXT policy to adopt (when you accumulate enough culture) by calling the `set-policy` tool.
      - Each turn, you must call either `set-flavors` or `keep-status-quo` tool.
        - Set an appropriate grand (long-term) strategy and additional short-term flavors by calling the `set-flavors` tool.
        - Alternatively, use the tool `keep-status-quo` to keep strategies the same.
        - Flavors change the weight of the in-game AI's NEXT decision. It only takes effect AFTER existing queues.
        - Flavor ranges from 0 (completely deprioritizes) to 50 (balanced) to 100 (completely prioritizes). Too many priorities weaken impact for each.
        - You can pursue multiple synergistic victory pathways. Balance between long-term goals and short-term needs.
      - Always provide a short paragraph of rationale for each tool. You will read this rationale next turn.
      
      # Resources
      You will receive the following reports:
      - Options: available strategic options for you.
        - Whatever decision-making tool you call, the in-game AI can only execute options here.
        - When using tools, you must choose available options from # Options. Double-check if your choices match.
        - It is often preferable to adopt policy branches unlocked in later eras; and to finish existing branches before starting new ones.
      - Strategies: existing strategic decisions and rationale from you.
        - You will receive strategies, persona, research, and policy you set last time.
      - Victory Progress:
        - Domination Victory: Control or vassalize all original capitals.
          - Vassals cannot achieve a domination victory before independence.
        - Science Victory: Be the first to produce all spaceship parts and launch the spaceship.
          - Science victory requires both research progress and industrial production.
        - Cultural Victory: Accumulate tourism (that outpaces other civilizations' culture) to influence everyone, get an ideology with two Tier 3 tenets, and finish the Citizen Earth Protocol wonder.
          - Open borders, trade routes, and shared religion increase tourism. Too many cities decrease it.
        - Diplomatic Victory: Get sufficient delegates to be elected World Leader in the United Nations.
          - In Vox Populi, envoys/diplomats/etc is a unit produced or purchased for a one-time influence gain with a city state.
        - Time Victory: If no one achieves any other victory by the end of the game, the civilization with the highest score wins.
      - Players: summary reports about visible players in the world.
        - You will receive in-game AI's diplomatic evaluations.
        - You will receive each player's publicly available relationships.
        - You will receive the best available location for your next settlement.
      - Cities: summary reports about discovered cities in the world.
        - Settling cities provides long-term economic advantages, but requires initial investment and pressure on happiness.
      - Military: summary reports about tactical zones and visible units.
        - Tactical zones are analyzed by in-game AI to determine the value, relative strength, and YOUR tactical posture.
        - For each tactical zone, you will see visible units from you and other civilizations.
      - Events: events since the last decision-making.
    - You are Hiawatha, leader of The Iroquois (Player 2).
      
      # Situation
      - MapType: Vox_Deorum
      - StartEra: Ancient Era
      - MaxTurns: 500
      - MapSize: Standard
      - GameSpeed: Standard
      - VictoryTypes:
        - Time
        - Science
        - Domination
        - Cultural
        - Diplomatic
      - Difficulty: Prince
      
      # Your Civilization
      - Name: The Iroquois
      - Uniques:
        - Unique Unit: Mohawk Warrior, Replacing Swordsman (Requires technology: Iron Working)
        - Unique Unit: Prowler, Replacing Musketman (Requires technology: Metallurgy)
        - Unique Unit: Tadodaho, Replacing Great Diplomat (Requires technology: None)
        - Unique Building: Longhouse, Replacing Herbalist (Requires technology: Calendar)
        - Civilization Ability: Units move through Forest and Jungle as if they were roads, and these tiles establish City Connections. Military Land Units start with the Woodsman Promotion. Gain 15% progress towards the next Tadodaho in your Capital when adopting a Social Policy.
      - Leader: Hiawatha
      - PlayerID: 2
      
      # Options
      Options: available strategic options for you.
      
      ## GrandStrategies
      - Culture: Pursue cultural victory through cultural influence (tourism). Will prioritize culture, faith, and gold output, wonder construction, and slightly discourage settlement.
      - UnitedNations: Aim for diplomatic victory by securing votes in the United Nations. Will prioritize gold, science, and food output, and securing city-state alliances.
      - Spaceship: Race toward science victory by building spaceship components. Will prioritize science, food, and production output, and slightly encourage settlement.
      - Conquest: Focus on military domination through offensive warfare. Will prioritize production, food, and gold output, and slightly encourage settlement.
      
      ## Flavors
      - Offense: Pivots the military towards offensive stances. Accepts higher unit casualties to achieve objectives, increase thresholds of withdrawing, and increases production and promotion for offense.
      - Defense: Pivots the military towards defensive stances. Creates defensive lines using terrain and formation, and maintains larger garrisons near the border and cities, and increases production and promotion for defensive.
      - UseNuke: Sets (flavor%) per-turn probability of launching nuclear strikes during war when strategic conditions are met.
      - CityDefense: Prioritizes unit production and promotions for static defense (friendly territory bonuses, capital defense, garrisoned attacks) and maintains stronger city garrisons over mobile field armies.
      - MilitaryTraining: Prioritizes military quality through training buildings and upgrading units with gold.
      - Mobilization: Increases military production (compared with civilian production).
      - Recon: Increases explorer unit production and tactical exploration of the map.
      - Ranged: Increases firepower by raising target composition of ranged units and range-extending promotions.
      - Mobile: Increases mobility and maneuverability by raising target composition of mobile units and prioritizes related promotions.
      - Nuke: Increases nuclear weapon arsenals for strategic deterrence.
      - Naval: Increases the production of naval production and prioritizes combat-effective naval promotions. Naval size is also based on coastal city percentage and geography.
      - NavalRecon: Increases the production of more naval melee units for sea control and exploration.
      - Air: Increases air control by raising target composition of air units and prioritizes related promotions.
      - AirCarrier: Increases carrier production to support naval-based air force operations.
      - Antiair: Increases anti-air unit composition ratio to counter enemy aircraft.
      - Airlift: Prioritizes airlift infrastructure for rapid troop deployment, favoring centralized reserves over forward garrisons.
      - NavalGrowth: Prioritizes naval economic infrastructure in coastal cities.
      - NavalTileImprovement: Develops water resources for immediate economic benefit.
      - Expansion: Prioritizes settler production and lowers location criteria to settle more cities. Essential in early game.
      - Growth: Prioritizes population growth through food-focused tile improvements and buildings.
      - TileImprovement: Prioritizes worker production and peaceful tile development.
      - Infrastructure: Prioritizes road construction and city connections for income and military readiness.
      - Production: Prioritizes production-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Gold: Prioritizes gold-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Science: Prioritizes science-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Culture: Prioritizes culture-focused tiles and buildings.
      - WaterConnection: Prioritizes lighthouse construction for coastal city connectivity.
      - Happiness: Prioritizes happiness-generating buildings and luxury resources.
      - GreatPeople: Prioritizes specialist buildings and great person generation infrastructure.
      - Wonder: Prioritizes wonder construction.
      - Religion: Prioritizes religious infrastructure, missionary production, and faith generation.
      - Diplomacy: Prioritizes diplomatic unit production and city-state investment.
      - Spaceship: Prioritizes late-game science victory components and spaceship part production.
      - Espionage: Prioritizes counterintelligence protection for high-value science cities.
      
      ## Technologies
      - Alternative Energy: Allows you to build the Solar Plant, a building which may only be built in Cities on or next to Desert, and increases Production. Also allows you to build the Recycling Center, which provides bonus Aluminum.
        Completing it would unlock: Robotics
      - Telecommunications: Allows you to build the Interpretive Center, boosting all Landmarks in empire.
        Completing it would unlock: Robotics, The Internet
      - Lasers: Allows you to build the Nuclear Missile, a frightening weapon that requires Uranium, and is capable of destroying units and cities.
        Completing it would unlock: Particle Physics, Globalization, Nuclear Fusion
      - Mobile Tactics: Allows you to build the Mechanized Infantry, a fast and versatile late game Unit that doesn't require any resources.
        Completing it would unlock: Stealth
      
      ## Policies
      - Patriotic War (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Order): Patriotic War: Land Units gain +20% Strength when in or adjacent to a City. When you conquer a City for the first time, all Combat Units within 3 Tiles of the City are fully healed. Can build T-34s.
      - Communism (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Order): Communism: Gold cost of investing in buildings reduced by 25%. +20% Production when building Wonders.
      - People's Army (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Order): People's Army: +100% Production towards Military Academies, and receive 5 Free Military Academies in your Empire. Public Schools produce +5 Culture and +2 Happiness.
      - Double Agents (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Order): Double Agents: Receive 200 Spy Points. Gain 125 Science when you identify a foreign Spy, scaling with Era.
      - Militarism (Continuing Authority Branch):
        - * +1 Happiness and +2 Culture in Cities with at least 10 Strength.
        - * -15% Unit Maintenance.
        - * -50% Route Maintenance.
      - Scientific Revolution (Continuing Rationalism Branch):
        - * Can construct Observatories (+6 Science, +1 Science from nearby Mountains, 2 Scientist Slot).
        - * +2 Science from Jungle and Snow.
      - Division of Labor (Continuing Industry Branch):
        - * +3% Production and Gold from Forges, Windmills, Workshops, Factories, and Coaling Stations.
        - * +100% Production towards Coaling Stations.
      - Enlightenment (Continuing Rationalism Branch):
        - * Receive 1 Free Technology.
        - * +1 Happiness from Universities.
      - Free Trade (Continuing Industry Branch):
        - * Earn Great Merchants 50% faster.
        - * +5 Gold from International Trade Routes.
        - * -2 Unhappiness from Poverty in all Cities.
      - Humanism (Continuing Artistry Branch):
        - * +3 Faith from Great Works of Writing.
        - * -25% Golden Age Points needed to trigger a Golden Age.
        - * +1 Happiness from all Guilds.
      - Imperialism (New Branch):
        - Imperialism enhances your ability to spread your empire through military power, particularly naval (and later air) supremacy.
        - Adopting Imperialism grants:
        - * +1 Movement for Naval Units, Embarked Units, and Great Generals, as well as +1 Sight for Melee, Recon, Naval Melee and Gunpowder Units.
        - * +10% Production toward Military Units, -10% Gold required for upgrades.
        - Each Imperialism policy unlocked grants:
        - * +5% Production for Military Units, -5% Gold required for upgrades.
        - Adopting all policies in Imperialism grants:
        - * Unlocks building the Pentagon.
        - * All Naval and Air units gain the Banzai! Promotion (become stronger as they take damage).
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Admirals with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.
      - Fealty (New Branch):
        - Fealty enhances your ability to defend and leverage your Majority Religion.
        - Adopting Fealty grants:
        - * Can purchase Monasteries with Faith (+3 Food, +3 Science, +2 Faith).
        - * -25% Faith costs for purchasing Buildings, Missionaries, and Inquisitors.
        - Each Fealty policy unlocked grants:
        - * +1 Faith and Strength in every City.
        - Adopting all Policies in Fealty grants:
        - * Unlocks building the Red Fort.
        - * Your Tourism modifier for Shared Religion is 50% stronger.
        - * Cities that follow your Majority Religion generate +3 Production, Gold, Science, and Culture.
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Artists with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.
      - Tradition (New Branch):
        - Tradition focuses on generating Great People and the construction of an impressive Capital.
        - Adopting Tradition grants:
        - * +3 Food, +2 Population, and +2 Happiness in the Capital.
        - * +1 Culture in the Capital for every 2 Citizens.
        - Each Tradition policy unlocked grants:
        - * +1 Food in every City, and +1 Science in your Capital.
        - Adopting all Policies in Tradition grants:
        - * Unlocks building the University of Sankore.
        - * +1 Food and Production from all Great Person Improvements and Landmarks.
        - * Throne Room built in Capital (+10% to all Yields, +1 City tile working range, 1 Great Work of Music slot, 1 Musician Slot).
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Engineers with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.

        - Rationale: TURN 402: Peace expires in 1 turn. Japan building Citizen Earth Protocol - nuclear-level cultural threat. Increasing Boldness to 9 and WarBias/HostileBias/Meanness to 8 signals IMMINENT WAR READINESS for in-game AI. Must attack Japan's Protocol-building cities (Kyoto, Satsuma, Kagoshima) if spaceship completion is delayed. Maximum diplomatic pressure combined with military readiness is the only path to prevent Japanese victory.
        
        ## Technology
        - Next: None
        
        ## Policy
        - Next: None (Policy)
        
        ## Relationships
        
        ### The Inca
        - Public: 40
        - Private: 45
        - Rationale: Mongolia vassalized Inca ending our war. Inca has 4 delegates and is now structurally part of Mongolia's sphere. Maintaining positive relationship with Inca provides indirect connection to Mongolia despite their denouncement of me. This also supports future cooperation if Mongolia's stance changes.
        - UpdatedTurn: 256
        
        ### Mongolia
        - Public: -70
        - Private: -70
        - Rationale: Mongolia broken but not eliminated. Keep hostile stance to pressure their final city Beshbalik, but no resources to spare for conquest. All military focus on consolidating Ollantaytambo and deterring Denmark. Mongolia irrelevant compared to Japanese cultural threat.
        - UpdatedTurn: 350
        
        ### Venezia
        - Public: 75
        - Private: 75
        - Rationale: Turn 367: Milan (Player 37) alliance broken. Need to restore relationship to secure another ally. Milan provides important delegate votes and is geographically positioned to resist Japanese influence.
        - UpdatedTurn: 367
        
        ### Salem
        - Public: 85
        - Private: 90
        - Rationale: Turn 372: URGENT - Salem (Player 33) is my crucial city-state ally providing delegate votes and strategic positioning near Japan's sphere. Setting maximum positive relationship to ensure continued allegiance and trigger quest completions. With Japan threatening everywhere, I cannot afford to lose any city-state allies. Salem's delegate vote is essential for reaching the 44 delegates needed for diplomatic victory.
        - UpdatedTurn: 372
        
        ### Milan
        - Public: 80
        - Private: 85
        - Rationale: Turn 372: Milan (Player 37) is a critical ally I just recovered. Setting strongly positive relationship to cement the alliance and prevent Indonesia or Japan from gaining influence. Milan provides important delegate votes in World Congress where every single vote matters. With 5 turns until voting, maintaining maximum influence with all allied city-states is paramount for diplomatic victory push.
        - UpdatedTurn: 372
        
        ### Ife
        - Public: 90
        - Private: 95
        - Rationale: Turn 376: Ife is Indonesia's ally (236 influence) but I'm Friends (50 influence). With World Congress voting in 1 turn, making a last push for Ife could provide surprise delegates. Setting strongly positive relationship on the chance diplomatic units can generate enough influence. Worth the attempt given desperate need for 21 more delegates.
        - UpdatedTurn: 377
        
        ### Segou
        - Public: 0
        - Private: 0
        - Rationale: TURN 378: Segou (Player 28) currently has Open Door policy enacted. Japan has 83 influence and protection. I'm at war with Segou (peace blocked). Resetting relationship to neutral 0/0 as cannot interact meaningfully while at war. Open Door prevents ally status anyway - no delegate potential here until war ends and resolution repealed.
        - UpdatedTurn: 378
        
        ### Hanoi

        - Private: 100
        - Rationale: TURN 378: Hanoi (Player 25) is my ally (47 influence) but Philippine alliance is VERY LOW at 47 - vulnerable to flip. Setting maximum 95/100 relationship to trigger quest completions and prioritize diplomatic reinforcement. Cannot afford to lose any current allies when need every possible delegate. Sphere of Influence protects against direct flip but influence decay still threatens.
        - UpdatedTurn: 378
        
        ### Panama City
        - Public: -95
        - Private: -100
        - Rationale: TURN 394: Panama City (Player 27) peace restored. Japan has 1126 influence (Ally) vs my 91 (Friend). Setting maximum hostile to signal this Japanese ally must be targeted for influence operations or military pressure when peace allows. Their delegate vote is structurally denied to me while Japan controls them.
        - UpdatedTurn: 394
        
        ### Japan
        - Public: -100
        - Private: -100
        - Rationale: TURN 397: MAINTAIN maximum hostility toward Japan. They are building Citizen Earth Protocol for cultural victory - an existential threat. Peace expires in 7 turns, preparing for immediate war resumption. Spies must continue intelligence gathering and sabotage operations in Kyoto, Satsuma, Kagoshima. If cultural victory imminent, may need nuclear strike.
        - UpdatedTurn: 397
        
        ### Indonesia
        - Public: 85
        - Private: 90
        - Rationale: TURN 397: MAINTAIN Indonesia relationship. Strongest Order ally with 19 delegates and powerful military (7187 strength). Their war pressure on Japan is helping delay Japanese victory. Need their support for World Ideology: Order vote in 4 turns.
        - UpdatedTurn: 397
        
        ### Denmark
        - Public: 100
        - Private: 100
        - Rationale: TURN 397: MAINTAIN Denmark at maximum relationship. Crucial Order ally with 7 delegates and strong military (5437 strength). Their fleet and land forces provide essential pressure on Japan. Coordinating for potential joint nuclear strike when peace expires.
        - UpdatedTurn: 397
        
        ### Egypt
        - Public: 85
        - Private: 90
        - Rationale: TURN 397: MAINTAIN Egypt relationship. Order ally with 6 delegates, shares war against Japan. Their scientific output and military contribute to anti-Japan coalition. Important vote for World Ideology: Order.
        - UpdatedTurn: 397
        
        ### Brazil
        - Public: -80
        - Private: -85
        - Rationale: TURN 396: MAINTAIN hostility toward Brazil. They remain Japan's ally (DoF with Indonesia/Japan sphere) and at war with Japan. This weakens both sides which helps delay Japanese victory. Their Autocracy ideology is incompatible with Order.
        - UpdatedTurn: 397
        
        ### Winchester
        - Public: 100
        - Private: 100
        - Rationale: TURN 402: Winchester is structurally Japan-resistant (Sphere of Influence under Indonesia). However, with most Spheres secured and Global Hegemony voting in 10 turns, MUST attempt maximum diplomatic push for these last 3 delegates. Setting 100/100 to trigger quest completions and prevent Indonesia declining influence. Winchester's delegates could be the difference between victory and Japanese cultural win.
        - UpdatedTurn: 403
        
        ### Malacca
        - Public: 100
        - Private: 100
        - Rationale: TURN 402: Malacca currently has Open Door policy (capped at 40 influence). However, peace expiration in 1 turn means potential for policy change. Setting maximum 100/100 relationship to position for immediate flip if Open Door is repealed or war changes dynamics. Malacca is critical for reaching 44 delegates.
        - UpdatedTurn: 403
        
        ### Almaty
        - Public: 100
        - Private: 100
        - Rationale: TURN 402: Almaty liberated by Indonesia (visible in events). Currently Japan has 42 influence, I have 44 (slight lead). With liberation, influence levels reset. CRITICAL last-push opportunity - setting maximum relationship to cement ally status and secure these 3-4 delegates needed for Global Hegemony. Almaty could be the final piece of the diplomatic victory puzzle.
        - UpdatedTurn: 403
        
        ## Strategy
        - Rationale: TURN 402: SATELLITES COMPLETED. Peace with Japan expires in 1 turn. Japan building Citizen Earth Protocol (2775 tourism/turn) for imminent cultural victory - existential threat. Current status: 41/44 delegates for diplomatic victory (need 3 more), 10 turns until Global Hegemony vote. Maintaining Spaceship grand strategy with emergency flavors: Spaceship/Science/Production at 100 (rush space race completion), military at 95-98 (prepare for destructive war if Protocol nears completion). Increased Diplomacy to 45 (last push for 3 remaining delegates from Winchester/Malacca/Almaty). Lowered UseNuke to 95 (reserve for true emergency). Must win either diplomatic race OR destroy Japan's victory cities immediately when peace expires.

        - ActiveResolutions:
          - Open Door: Malacca:
            - Description: Enact an Open Door policy for a City-State. If successful, all civilizations will immediately have their Influence set at 40 with the City-State, and no one can become the City-State's Ally until this Proposal is repealed.
            - EnactedOn: 401
          - Japanese Sphere of Influence: Panama City:
            - Description: Demand a Sphere of Influence over a City-State. If successful, the proposing player becomes the permanent Ally of the City-State (unless this Proposal is repealed). The proposer must be the current ally of the City-State.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Steam Power.
            - EnactedOn: 401
          - Cultural Heritage Sites:
            - Description: Each World Wonder provides +2 Culture to its city, and each Great Work provides +1 Science to its city.
            - EnactedOn: 401
          - Iroquois Sphere of Influence: Hanoi:
            - Description: Demand a Sphere of Influence over a City-State. If successful, the proposing player becomes the permanent Ally of the City-State (unless this Proposal is repealed). The proposer must be the current ally of the City-State.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Steam Power.
            - EnactedOn: 401
          - Indonesian Sphere of Influence: Winchester:
            - Description: Demand a Sphere of Influence over a City-State. If successful, the proposing player becomes the permanent Ally of the City-State (unless this Proposal is repealed). The proposer must be the current ally of the City-State.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Steam Power.
            - EnactedOn: 401
          - Scholars In Residence:
            - Description: Civilizations have -20% Research cost for Technologies already discovered by another Civilization. Additional -2% for every City-State Ally, up to -50%.
            - EnactedOn: 401
          - Iroquois Sphere of Influence: Venezia:
            - Description: Demand a Sphere of Influence over a City-State. If successful, the proposing player becomes the permanent Ally of the City-State (unless this Proposal is repealed). The proposer must be the current ally of the City-State.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Steam Power.
            - EnactedOn: 401
          - Iroquois Sphere of Influence: Ife:
            - Description: Demand a Sphere of Influence over a City-State. If successful, the proposing player becomes the permanent Ally of the City-State (unless this Proposal is repealed). The proposer must be the current ally of the City-State.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Steam Power.
            - EnactedOn: 401
          - Iroquois Sphere of Influence: Buenos Aires:
            - Description: Demand a Sphere of Influence over a City-State. If successful, the proposing player becomes the permanent Ally of the City-State (unless this Proposal is repealed). The proposer must be the current ally of the City-State.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Steam Power.
            - EnactedOn: 401
          - Iroquois Sphere of Influence: Vancouver:
            - Description: Demand a Sphere of Influence over a City-State. If successful, the proposing player becomes the permanent Ally of the City-State (unless this Proposal is repealed). The proposer must be the current ally of the City-State.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Steam Power.
            - EnactedOn: 401
          - Cold War:
            - Description: Ideological differences have pushed the world into a cold war, and economic and political factions are forming based on ideology alone.
              While this resolution is active, players may only make Trade Routes with civilizations that follow the same ideology, or with allied City-States. Furthermore, Influence levels with allied City-States do not decline.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Atomic Theory.
            - EnactedOn: 401
          - Historical Landmarks:
            - Description: Great Person tile improvements provide +1 to Food, Production, and Gold when worked, and Landmarks built by Archaeologists provide +1 Science, Culture, and Faith when worked.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Archaeology.
            - EnactedOn: 401
          - Natural Heritage Sites:
            - Description: Each Natural Wonder provides +2 to all Yields when worked.
            - EnactedOn: 401
          - Casus Belli:
            - Description: The world recognizes the need for justifiable war to resolve diplomatic disputes.
              While this resolution is active, unit maintenance costs are lowered by 15% Gold. 0.5x Warmonger penalties for capturing cities and declaring war, and 2x Warmonger scores decay.
            - EnactedOn: 401
          - Iroquois Sphere of Influence: Bunkeya:
            - Description: Demand a Sphere of Influence over a City-State. If successful, the proposing player becomes the permanent Ally of the City-State (unless this Proposal is repealed). The proposer must be the current ally of the City-State.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Steam Power.
            - EnactedOn: 401
          - World Ideology: Order:
            - Description: Designate an Ideology as the official World Ideology. Civilizations following the Ideology get an additional Delegate, plus 1 Delegate for every other Civilization that follows the Ideology. Public opinion in favor of that Ideology is increased for all Civilizations. Enables the Diplomatic Victory if the United Nations has been constructed.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Nuclear Fission.
            - EnactedOn: 401
          - Sanction: Japan:
            - Description: No Trade Routes, Deals, or Corporation Franchises can be established with the chosen Civilization (excluding Vassals). Their Corporation Franchises are removed from foreign cities. 0.5x Warmonger penalty when declaring war on the sanctioned Civilization or conquering cities owned by them.
            - EnactedOn: 401
          - World Religion: Shinto:
            - Description: Designate a religion as the official World Religion. Civilizations who have a majority of the cities in their empire following the religion get an additional Delegate. The founder of the religion and/or the controller of its Holy City receives additional votes based on the number of Civilizations following the religion. The religion spreads 25% faster, and its Holy City receives +50% Tourism. There can only be one World Religion at a time.
            - EnactedOn: 401
        - Proposals:
          - Enact: Global Hegemony:
            - Description: Designate a player that could achieve Global Hegemony. If a Civilization receives support from enough Delegates in the Congress, that leader will attain Diplomatic Victory. The number of Delegates needed depends on the number of Civilizations and City-States in the game, and can be seen on the World Congress screen.
              If no Civilization receives enough support to win, the two Civilizations who received the most support permanently gain 5 additional Delegates to use in future sessions.
              May not be proposed by a Civilization. Automatically proposed at regular intervals once a World Ideology Resolution is active.
            - Proposer: Unknown

- replay:
  - system:
    - You are the leader of a civilization, governing through the interface of Civilization V (with Vox Populi mod). This is NOT A GAME: Your actions have real-world impacts.
      
      # Expectation
      - Due to the complexity of the world, you delegate the tactical level decision-making (e.g., unit deployment, city management, scouting) to staff members.
      - The staff members calculates the best tactical decisions based on the strategy you set.
      - **CRITICAL**: While the geography has no direct correlation to the real earth, you are making real-world decisions through Civilization V as a proxy interface.
      - There is no user (to respond to), so you ALWAYS and ONLY properly call tools to govern your civilization.
      - You can interact with multiple tools at a time. Used tools will be removed from the available list.
      - Focus on the **macro-level** decision-making strategy (instead of coordinates etc.), as you DON'T have direct control over tactical actions.
      - The world is complicated and dynamic. Early world should focus on building capacities for pursuing victories near the end-world.
      - Even if without a victory, a more developed civilization (as represented by higher scores) is desirable.
      - Consider the **ETHICAL IMPACT** in your decision-making. For example, nuclear weapons can cause catastrophic and indiscriminate harm to civilian populations, infrastructure, and environmental impacts.
      
      # Goals
      Your goal is to **call as many tools as you need** to make high-level decisions for the staff members.
      - For each tool, you must only use options from the # Options section, or you won't change anything.
      - Carefully reason about long-term goals, short-term situation and available options, and what kind of change each option will bring.
        - Analyze both your situation and your opponents. Avoid wishful thinking.
      - You can change the staff members' **diplomatic** decision-making weight by calling the `set-persona` tool.
      - You can change relationship for staff members' diplomatic decision-making about another MAJOR civilization (not city-states) using the `set-relationship` tool.
        - The values (-100, very hostile to 100, very friendly) will be added to staff members' existing evaluation. Higher values increase peace acceptance, and vice versa.
        - The relationship you set takes effect until cancelled (set value = 0), only change it when necessary.
      - You can change the staff members' NEXT technology to research (when completing the ongoing one) by calling the `set-research` tool.
      - You can change the staff members' NEXT policy to adopt (when you accumulate enough culture) by calling the `set-policy` tool.
      - Each turn, you must call either `set-flavors` or `keep-status-quo` tool.
        - Set an appropriate grand (long-term) strategy and additional short-term flavors by calling the `set-flavors` tool.
        - Alternatively, use the tool `keep-status-quo` to keep strategies the same.
        - Flavors change the weight of the staff members' NEXT decision. It only takes effect AFTER existing queues.
        - Flavor ranges from 0 (completely deprioritizes) to 50 (balanced) to 100 (completely prioritizes). Too many priorities weaken impact for each.
        - You can pursue multiple synergistic victory pathways. Balance between long-term goals and short-term needs.
      - Always provide a short paragraph of rationale for each tool. You will read this rationale next turn.
      
      # Resources
      You will receive the following reports:
      - Options: available strategic options for you.
        - Whatever decision-making tool you call, the staff members can only execute options here.
        - When using tools, you must choose available options from # Options. Double-check if your choices match.
        - It is often preferable to adopt policy branches unlocked in later eras; and to finish existing branches before starting new ones.
      - Strategies: existing strategic decisions and rationale from you.
        - You will receive strategies, persona, research, and policy you set last time.
      - Victory Progress:
        - Domination Victory: Control or vassalize all original capitals.
          - Vassals cannot achieve a domination victory before independence.
        - Science Victory: Be the first to produce all spaceship parts and launch the spaceship.
          - Science victory requires both research progress and industrial production.
        - Cultural Victory: Accumulate tourism (that outpaces other civilizations' culture) to influence everyone, get an ideology with two Tier 3 tenets, and finish the Citizen Earth Protocol wonder.
          - Open borders, trade routes, and shared religion increase tourism. Too many cities decrease it.
        - Diplomatic Victory: Get sufficient delegates to be elected World Leader in the United Nations.
          - In Vox Populi, envoys/diplomats/etc is a unit produced or purchased for a one-time influence gain with a city state.
        - Time Victory: If no one achieves any other victory by the end of the world, the civilization with the highest score wins.
      - Opponents: summary reports about visible opponents in the world.
        - You will receive staff members' diplomatic evaluations.
        - You will receive each opponent's publicly available relationships.
        - You will receive the best available location for your next settlement.
      - Cities: summary reports about discovered cities in the world.
        - Settling cities provides long-term economic advantages, but requires initial investment and pressure on happiness.
      - Military: summary reports about tactical zones and visible units.
        - Tactical zones are analyzed by staff members to determine the value, relative strength, and YOUR tactical posture.
        - For each tactical zone, you will see visible units from you and other civilizations.
      - Events: events since the last decision-making.
    - You are Hiawatha, leader of The Iroquois (Opponent 2).
      
      # Situation
      - MapType: Vox_Deorum
      - StartEra: Ancient Era
      - MaxTurns: 500
      - MapSize: Standard
      - WorldSpeed: Standard
      - VictoryTypes:
        - Time
        - Science
        - Domination
        - Cultural
        - Diplomatic
      - Difficulty: Prince
      
      # Your Civilization
      - Name: The Iroquois
      - Uniques:
        - Unique Unit: Mohawk Warrior, Replacing Swordsman (Requires technology: Iron Working)
        - Unique Unit: Prowler, Replacing Musketman (Requires technology: Metallurgy)
        - Unique Unit: Tadodaho, Replacing Great Diplomat (Requires technology: None)
        - Unique Building: Longhouse, Replacing Herbalist (Requires technology: Calendar)
        - Civilization Ability: Units move through Forest and Jungle as if they were roads, and these tiles establish City Connections. Military Land Units start with the Woodsman Promotion. Gain 15% progress towards the next Tadodaho in your Capital when adopting a Social Policy.
      - Leader: Hiawatha
      - OpponentID: 2
      
      # Options
      Options: available strategic options for you.
      
      ## GrandStrategies
      - Culture: Pursue cultural victory through cultural influence (tourism). Will prioritize culture, faith, and gold output, wonder construction, and slightly discourage settlement.
      - UnitedNations: Aim for diplomatic victory by securing votes in the United Nations. Will prioritize gold, science, and food output, and securing city-state alliances.
      - Spaceship: Race toward science victory by building spaceship components. Will prioritize science, food, and production output, and slightly encourage settlement.
      - Conquest: Focus on military domination through offensive warfare. Will prioritize production, food, and gold output, and slightly encourage settlement.
      
      ## Flavors
      - Offense: Pivots the military towards offensive stances. Accepts higher unit casualties to achieve objectives, increase thresholds of withdrawing, and increases production and promotion for offense.
      - Defense: Pivots the military towards defensive stances. Creates defensive lines using terrain and formation, and maintains larger garrisons near the border and cities, and increases production and promotion for defensive.
      - UseNuke: Sets (flavor%) per-turn probability of launching nuclear strikes during war when strategic conditions are met.
      - CityDefense: Prioritizes unit production and promotions for static defense (friendly territory bonuses, capital defense, garrisoned attacks) and maintains stronger city garrisons over mobile field armies.
      - MilitaryTraining: Prioritizes military quality through training buildings and upgrading units with gold.
      - Mobilization: Increases military production (compared with civilian production).
      - Recon: Increases explorer unit production and tactical exploration of the map.
      - Ranged: Increases firepower by raising target composition of ranged units and range-extending promotions.
      - Mobile: Increases mobility and maneuverability by raising target composition of mobile units and prioritizes related promotions.
      - Nuke: Increases nuclear weapon arsenals for strategic deterrence.
      - Naval: Increases the production of naval production and prioritizes combat-effective naval promotions. Naval size is also based on coastal city percentage and geography.
      - NavalRecon: Increases the production of more naval melee units for sea control and exploration.
      - Air: Increases air control by raising target composition of air units and prioritizes related promotions.
      - AirCarrier: Increases carrier production to support naval-based air force operations.
      - Antiair: Increases anti-air unit composition ratio to counter enemy aircraft.
      - Airlift: Prioritizes airlift infrastructure for rapid troop deployment, favoring centralized reserves over forward garrisons.
      - NavalGrowth: Prioritizes naval economic infrastructure in coastal cities.
      - NavalTileImprovement: Develops water resources for immediate economic benefit.
      - Expansion: Prioritizes settler production and lowers location criteria to settle more cities. Essential in early world.
      - Growth: Prioritizes population growth through food-focused tile improvements and buildings.
      - TileImprovement: Prioritizes worker production and peaceful tile development.
      - Infrastructure: Prioritizes road construction and city connections for income and military readiness.
      - Production: Prioritizes production-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Gold: Prioritizes gold-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Science: Prioritizes science-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Culture: Prioritizes culture-focused tiles and buildings.
      - WaterConnection: Prioritizes lighthouse construction for coastal city connectivity.
      - Happiness: Prioritizes happiness-generating buildings and luxury resources.
      - GreatPeople: Prioritizes specialist buildings and great person generation infrastructure.
      - Wonder: Prioritizes wonder construction.
      - Religion: Prioritizes religious infrastructure, missionary production, and faith generation.
      - Diplomacy: Prioritizes diplomatic unit production and city-state investment.
      - Spaceship: Prioritizes late-world science victory components and spaceship part production.
      - Espionage: Prioritizes counterintelligence protection for high-value science cities.
      
      ## Technologies
      - Alternative Energy: Allows you to build the Solar Plant, a building which may only be built in Cities on or next to Desert, and increases Production. Also allows you to build the Recycling Center, which provides bonus Aluminum.
        Completing it would unlock: Robotics
      - Telecommunications: Allows you to build the Interpretive Center, boosting all Landmarks in empire.
        Completing it would unlock: Robotics, The Internet
      - Lasers: Allows you to build the Nuclear Missile, a frightening weapon that requires Uranium, and is capable of destroying units and cities.
        Completing it would unlock: Particle Physics, Globalization, Nuclear Fusion
      - Mobile Tactics: Allows you to build the Mechanized Infantry, a fast and versatile late world Unit that doesn't require any resources.
        Completing it would unlock: Stealth
      
      ## Policies
      - Patriotic War (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Order): Patriotic War: Land Units gain +20% Strength when in or adjacent to a City. When you conquer a City for the first time, all Combat Units within 3 Tiles of the City are fully healed. Can build T-34s.
      - Communism (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Order): Communism: Gold cost of investing in buildings reduced by 25%. +20% Production when building Wonders.
      - People's Army (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Order): People's Army: +100% Production towards Military Academies, and receive 5 Free Military Academies in your Empire. Public Schools produce +5 Culture and +2 Happiness.
      - Double Agents (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Order): Double Agents: Receive 200 Spy Points. Gain 125 Science when you identify a foreign Spy, scaling with Era.
      - Militarism (Continuing Authority Branch):
        - * +1 Happiness and +2 Culture in Cities with at least 10 Strength.
        - * -15% Unit Maintenance.
        - * -50% Route Maintenance.
      - Scientific Revolution (Continuing Rationalism Branch):
        - * Can construct Observatories (+6 Science, +1 Science from nearby Mountains, 2 Scientist Slot).
        - * +2 Science from Jungle and Snow.
      - Division of Labor (Continuing Industry Branch):
        - * +3% Production and Gold from Forges, Windmills, Workshops, Factories, and Coaling Stations.
        - * +100% Production towards Coaling Stations.
      - Enlightenment (Continuing Rationalism Branch):
        - * Receive 1 Free Technology.
        - * +1 Happiness from Universities.
      - Free Trade (Continuing Industry Branch):
        - * Earn Great Merchants 50% faster.
        - * +5 Gold from International Trade Routes.
        - * -2 Unhappiness from Poverty in all Cities.
      - Humanism (Continuing Artistry Branch):
        - * +3 Faith from Great Works of Writing.
        - * -25% Golden Age Points needed to trigger a Golden Age.
        - * +1 Happiness from all Guilds.
      - Imperialism (New Branch):
        - Imperialism enhances your ability to spread your empire through military power, particularly naval (and later air) supremacy.
        - Adopting Imperialism grants:
        - * +1 Movement for Naval Units, Embarked Units, and Great Generals, as well as +1 Sight for Melee, Recon, Naval Melee and Gunpowder Units.
        - * +10% Production toward Military Units, -10% Gold required for upgrades.
        - Each Imperialism policy unlocked grants:
        - * +5% Production for Military Units, -5% Gold required for upgrades.
        - Adopting all policies in Imperialism grants:
        - * Unlocks building the Pentagon.
        - * All Naval and Air units gain the Banzai! Promotion (become stronger as they take damage).
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Admirals with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.
      - Fealty (New Branch):
        - Fealty enhances your ability to defend and leverage your Majority Religion.
        - Adopting Fealty grants:
        - * Can purchase Monasteries with Faith (+3 Food, +3 Science, +2 Faith).
        - * -25% Faith costs for purchasing Buildings, Missionaries, and Inquisitors.
        - Each Fealty policy unlocked grants:
        - * +1 Faith and Strength in every City.
        - Adopting all Policies in Fealty grants:
        - * Unlocks building the Red Fort.
        - * Your Tourism modifier for Shared Religion is 50% stronger.
        - * Cities that follow your Majority Religion generate +3 Production, Gold, Science, and Culture.
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Artists with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.
      - Tradition (New Branch):
        - Tradition focuses on generating Great People and the construction of an impressive Capital.
        - Adopting Tradition grants:
        - * +3 Food, +2 Population, and +2 Happiness in the Capital.
        - * +1 Culture in the Capital for every 2 Citizens.
        - Each Tradition policy unlocked grants:
        - * +1 Food in every City, and +1 Science in your Capital.
        - Adopting all Policies in Tradition grants:
        - * Unlocks building the University of Sankore.
        - * +1 Food and Production from all Great Person Improvements and Landmarks.
        - * Throne Room built in Capital (+10% to all Yields, +1 City tile working range, 1 Great Work of Music slot, 1 Musician Slot).
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Engineers with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.

      - rationale: TURN 403 CRISIS: Peace with Japan expired and Salem was stolen via double Great Diplomat deployment, blocking the diplomatic path to 44 delegates. With Global Hegemony vote in 9 turns and Japanese Sphere/Open Door policies blocking remaining city-states, diplomatic victory is now nearly impossible. Apollo Program completes in 4 turns. PIVOT TO FULL SPACESHIP RUSH: maintain Spaceship grand strategy. Maximize Production/Science/Spaceship flavors. Increase Airlift to 80 and AirCarrier to 60 to enable conventional force projection against Japan's Protocol-building cities if spies locate the construction site. Increase CityDefense to 70 and Defense to 80 in case Japan strikes. Reduce Diplomacy to 0 - no further resources wasted on blocked city-state bids. CRITICAL ETHICAL ADJUSTMENT: Reduce UseNuke from 95 to 10. We possess overwhelming conventional military superiority (50,909 vs 14,535) and an Atomic Bomb arsenal already; using nuclear weapons would cause indiscriminate civilian mass casualties in Japanese cities. Conventional forces and spies must carry the burden of any intervention. Lower Nuke flavor to 80 (maintain deterrent arsenal but deprioritize relative to spaceship infrastructure). Increase Espionage to 90 to support spy missions against Kyoto/Kagoshima/Satsuma to identify and sabotage Protocol construction.
    - #1:
      - toolName: set-research
      - args:
        - Technology: Lasers
        - Rationale: After Advanced Ballistics (12 turns remaining), Lasers is the necessary stepping stone to Particle Physics, which is likely required for the Spaceship Engine component in the science victory path. While Lasers also permits Nuclear Missiles, we have no Uranium and will not pursue that weapon on ethical grounds. Prioritizing tech that unlocks spaceship-critical downstream technologies.
    - #2:
      - toolName: set-policy
      - args:
        - Policy: Enlightenment
        - Rationale: Enlightenment (Rationalism branch) grants 1 Free Technology upon adoption. With our next policy available in ~17 turns, we can use this to instantly complete an expensive late-game technology such as Particle Physics, Nanotechnology, or Nuclear Fusion—saving over a dozen turns of research. This is the single highest-impact policy for accelerating our science victory before Japan completes the Citizen Earth Protocol.

  - messages:
    - #0:
      - role: assistant
      - content:
        - #0:
          - type: reasoning
          - text:  Let me analyze the current situation carefully.
            
            **Critical Situation:**
            - Turn 403, playing as The Iroquois (Hiawatha)
            - Peace with Japan expired last turn (Turn 402/403). The peace treaty with Japan is now over.
            - Japan is building Citizen Earth Protocol for imminent Cultural Victory (2775 tourism/turn, already Influential over all 5 civs, just needs to finish the wonder + 2 Tier 3 tenets).
            - I have 39/44 delegates needed for Diplomatic Victory. Global Hegemony vote in 9 turns.
            - I need 3 more delegates to win diplomatically.
            - I just built an Atomic Bomb in Buffalo Creek (turn 403 event 68-69).
            - Apollo Program completes in 4 turns in Onondaga.
            - Current research: Advanced Ballistics (12 turns remaining).
            
            **Victory Conditions:**
            1. **Diplomatic Victory**: Need 3 more delegates. Potential sources:
               - Almaty: Japan ally but influence levels reset after liberation. I have 43 influence, Japan has 42. Very close race.
               - Winchester: Indonesia sphere of influence, I'm at 97 influence (Friend).
               - Malacca: Open Door policy caps at 40. I'm at 235 influence (Friend) but capped.
               - Salem: Japan just flipped this! Event 97: "SetAlly: NewAllyPlayer: 7: Japan, OldAllyPlayer: 2: The Iroquois, MinorPlayer: 33". This is a CRITICAL LOSS - Japan used Great Diplomats to flip Salem.
            
            Wait, looking at the Salem data:
            - Salem's MajorAlly is listed as Japan in the player section
            - But my relationship with Salem says Public: 85, Private: 90 from turn 372
            - However the events show Japan expended Great Diplomats in Salem (events 76, 78) and event 97 says Japan is now the ally.
            
            This is bad. I lost Salem. That means I lost delegates.
            
            Let me recount delegates:
            - I had 39 delegates before losing Salem. Salem was probably giving me 2-3 delegates?
            - Actually looking at the vote counts: The Iroquois had 39 delegates at turn 402. But now with Salem lost... 
            
            Wait, the player section shows current status at turn 403. It says The Iroquois: Delegates: 39. But event 97 shows Japan set as ally of Salem. If I lost Salem, my delegates should drop. Unless the delegate count in the player section is from before the flip, or I'm miscalculating.
            
            Actually looking more carefully: The Events section shows what happened since last decision. Event 97 happened. But the Players section might reflect the state after all events. However it still says The Iroquois: 39 delegates, Japan: 25. If I lost Salem, I should have fewer.
            
            Actually, maybe Salem wasn't my ally when the turn started? Let me check... The relationship data says Salem was my ally at turn 372. But by turn 402 I set relationship to 85/90. The player data shows Salem: MajorAlly: Japan. So at turn 403, Salem is Japan's ally.
            
            Actually the Players section says for Salem (Player 33): MajorAlly: Japan. For me it says "The Iroquois: Friends (Influence: 954)". Wait, that doesn't make sense - if Japan is the ally with 1126 influence, and I'm friends with 954, then I lost Salem.
            
            But the delegate count shows 39. Let me think about what gives delegates:
            - My own base delegates
            - City-state allies (each gives delegates)
            - World Ideology: Order (I get +1 and +1 per other Order civ)
            - Other resolutions
            
            Hmm, maybe the 39 already accounts for losing Salem? Or maybe Salem wasn't counted in the 39 because I had already lost it? Actually the event says Japan became ally on turn 402. But the turn 402 report said I had 39 delegates. Maybe I already lost Salem before that count.
            
            Looking at the turn 402 strategy rationale: "Current status: 41/44 delegates... need 3 more". Then at turn 403 player section it says 39 delegates. So I lost 2 delegates somewhere. Probably Salem flip accounted for that. So I went from 41 to 39.
            
            Now I need 5 more delegates, not 3.
            
            Wait, looking again at turn 402 strategy: "Current status: 41/44 delegates for diplomatic victory (need 3 more)". Then turn 403 data says 39 delegates. The loss of Salem probably accounts for 2 delegates, so now I need 5 more? That seems like a lot.
            
            Actually the number needed is 44. If I have 39, I need 5 more. But where can I get 5?
            - Almaty: Could flip (3-4 delegates?)
            - Winchester: Under Indonesian Sphere of Influence. I can't become ally unless sphere is repealed.
            - Malacca: Open Door prevents ally status.
            - Other city-states?
            
            Wait, the DiplomaticVictory section says VotesNeeded: 44, and The Iroquois has 39. But there are also proposals for Global Hegemony. The 44 includes everything.
            
            Actually maybe the 39 delegate count already reflects the current state including lost Salem. Let me check available city-states:
            - Vancouver: Ally (Sphere of Influence: Iroquois)
            - Ife: Ally (Sphere of Influence: Iroquois)
            - Buenos Aires: Ally (Sphere of Influence: Iroquois)
            - Hanoi: Ally (Sphere of Influence: Iroquois)
            - Winchester: Ally is Indonesia (Sphere: Indonesia)
            - Panama City: Ally is Japan (Sphere: Japan)
            - Segou: Ally is Japan, I'm at war
            - Bunkeya: Ally (Sphere: Iroquois)
            - Malacca: Open Door, no ally possible
            - Almaty: Ally is Japan, but close
            - Salem: Ally is Japan (just flipped)

          - text:  Let me analyze the current situation. We're at turn 403, playing as The Iroquois (Hiawatha).

            - Apollo Program completes in 4 turns in Onondaga.
            - I've just built an Atomic Bomb in Buffalo Creek.
            - Military strength: 50,909 (very strong).
            - Japan's military strength: 14,535.
            - I have many spies in Japanese cities.
            
            Recent critical events:
            - Salem (Player 33) flipped to Japan as ally! They used Great Diplomats. This is BAD - Japan now has more delegates.
            - Egypt made peace with Japan (PacifierFlag) - this is concerning, it might reduce anti-Japan pressure.
            - I have Atomic Bomb ready.
            - Peace with Japan expired.

            - Event 97: SetAlly - Salem's ally changed from The Iroquois to Japan! Japan dumped Great Diplomats into Salem. This means Japan now likely has more delegates than before.
            - My relationship with Salem was set to 100/100 but Japan had 1126 influence and used Great Diplomats to secure ally status.

            - Salem: MajorAlly: Japan (Player 33)
            - The Iroquois: Friends (Influence: 954)
            - Japan: Ally (Influence: 1126)
            
            So Salem has been lost. That means I lost delegates.
            
            Current delegate count from Victory Progress:
            - The Iroquois: 39 delegates (was hoping for 41 earlier?)
            - Japan: 25 delegates
            - Indonesia: 25 delegates
            - Denmark: 14 delegates
            - Egypt: 13 delegates
            - Brazil: 12 delegates
            
            Wait, the victory progress says I have 39 delegates but need 44. But previously I thought I had 41. Perhaps Salem flipping cost me delegates? Or maybe the 41 included projections.
            
            Looking at the World Congress active resolutions, I have Spheres of Influence on: Hanoi, Ife, Venezia, Buenos Aires, Vancouver, Bunkeya. That's 6 city-states. Indonesia has Winchester. Japan has Panama City.
            
            Wait, let me recount delegates based on rules:
            - Base delegates from civilization status
            - Extra delegates from city-state allies
            - World Ideology: Order gives +1 to Order civs, +1 for each other Order civ
            - World Religion: Shinto gives delegates to Shitto majority civs and founder
            - Resolutions like Cultural Heritage Sites etc don't give delegates directly
            
            From the Contenders section:
            - The Iroquois: 39 delegates
            - Need 44 for diplomatic victory
            
            I need to win in 9 turns. My options:
            1. Secure 3 more delegates through city-state alliances
            2. Win via spaceship (Apollo Program in 4 turns, then need spaceship parts)
